# Generate A Polynomial Pools Matrix

Construct a binary pooling matrix by the Polynomial Pools (PP)
algorithm, using polynomial evaluation over \\\mathrm{GF}(q)\\ to define
the pool incidence pattern.

## Usage

``` r
pp_matrix(q, d, nl, N = NULL)
```

## Arguments

- q:

  Integer scalar giving the Galois-field order. It must be a prime power
  so that \\\mathrm{GF}(q)\\ exists. This parameter controls the number
  of pools per layer and the coordinate alphabet used by the PP
  construction.

- d:

  Integer scalar giving the PP dimension. It must satisfy \\d \ge 2\\.
  The full untruncated design has \\N\_{\max} = q^d\\ individuals and
  canonical pool size \\q^{d-1}\\.

- nl:

  Integer scalar giving the number of layers, written \\n_l\\ in the PP
  literature. It must satisfy \\1 \le n_l \le q + 1\\. The returned
  design has \\J = n_l q\\ pools and each individual is assigned to
  exactly \\n_l\\ pools.

- N:

  Integer scalar giving the number of individuals actually retained in
  the matrix. The default is \\N = q^d\\. It must satisfy \\1 \le N \le
  q^d\\ and \\q \mid N\\. Values smaller than \\q^d\\ truncate the full
  PP design to its first \\N\\ columns.

## Value

A sparse binary matrix of class `dgCMatrix` with dimensions \\J \times
N\\, where \\J = n_l q\\. Entry \\M\_{ji} = 1\\ indicates that
individual \\i\\ belongs to pool \\j\\. The matrix carries attribute
`"pp"` containing a named list with:

- q:

  Integer; field order

- d:

  Integer; dimension

- nl:

  Integer; number of layers

- N:

  Integer; number of samples

- M:

  Integer; legacy display alias for the number of pools \\J\\

- pool_size:

  Integer; actual number of samples per pool (first row-sum after any
  \\N \< q^d\\ truncation; equals \\q^{d-1}\\ only when \\N = q^d\\)

- k_max:

  Integer; maximum detectable positives with COMP

In the full untruncated design, all column sums equal \\n_l\\ and all
row sums equal \\q^{d-1}\\. Under truncation, the column sums remain
\\w_i = \|\mathcal{J}\_i\| = n_l\\, while the row sums may differ
slightly from \\q^{d-1}\\ near the truncation boundary.

## Details

Let \\\mathbf{M} = (M\_{ji}) \in \\0,1\\^{J \times N}\\ denote the
returned pooling matrix, where \\j \in \\1, \ldots, J\\\\ indexes pools
and \\i \in \\1, \ldots, N\\\\ indexes individuals. The PP construction
uses polynomial evaluation over \\\mathrm{GF}(q)\\. The main derived
quantities are \$\$N\_{\max} = q^d, \qquad J = n_l q, \qquad k\_{\max} =
\left\lfloor \frac{n_l - 1}{d - 1} \right\rfloor.\$\$

Individuals are indexed by field-valued coordinates and assigned to
pools by polynomial relations over \\\mathrm{GF}(q)\\. In the full
untruncated design, each individual appears in exactly \\n_l\\ pools,
each pool contains exactly \\q^{d-1}\\ individuals, and any two
individuals share at most \\d - 1\\ pools.

When \\N \< q^d\\, the constructor truncates the full design to its
first \\N\\ columns. The column weights remain \\w_i =
\|\mathcal{J}\_i\| = n_l\\, while row sizes may vary slightly near the
truncation boundary.

The standard PP-based P-BEST matrix family uses \\q = 8\\, \\d = 3\\,
\\n_l = 6\\, and \\N = 384\\, giving \\J = 48\\ pools and \\k\_{\max} =
2\\ under the PP COMP detection bound.

Long derivations and family context are developed in the package
vignettes, especially `ppgt_pp` and `ppgt_pbest`.

## PP Structure

In the full untruncated design, all column sums equal \\n_l\\ and all
row sums equal \\q^{d-1}\\. Pools within each layer are disjoint, and
the intersection bound implies that COMP can separate negatives whenever
the number of positives stays below the PP detection limit.

## P-BEST Context

The package documentation uses the term "PP-based P-BEST" for the \\q =
8\\, \\d = 3\\, \\n_l = 6\\, \\N = 384\\ matrix family. That matrix
family is distinct from later package-specific clinical helpers and
should not be read as a blanket claim about every P-BEST protocol layer.

## References

Tan, Y. H. I. (2020). Pooling matrix designs for group testing. *SIAM
Undergraduate Research Online*, 13, 1-21.

Shental, N., Levy, S., Wuvshet, V., et al. (2020). Efficient
high-throughput SARS-CoV-2 testing to detect asymptomatic carriers.
*Science Advances*, 6(37), eabc5961.

Brust, D., & Brust, J. J. (2023). Effective matrix designs for COVID-19
group testing. *BMC Bioinformatics*, 24, Article 26.

[`pp_verify()`](https://myaseen208.github.io/ppgt/reference/pp_verify.md)

## Examples

``` r
# P-BEST standard design: 48 pools x 384 samples
M_pbest <- pp_matrix(q = 8, d = 3, nl = 6, N = 384)
M_pbest
```
